name: explain-code
description: Explain or summarize code written in C, C++, Java or Rust for a reader who knows C# and TypeScript. Covers CMakeLists.txt, Makefile, pom.xml, build.gradle and Cargo.toml. Use when the user asks to explain, read, summarize or review code in C, C++, Java or Rust.
license: MIT

# Explain code in C, C++, Java or Rust

## Rules

1. Explain what the code does. Do not explain what its names suggest.
2. Never invent a definition. When the excerpt does not show something, write "not visible in this excerpt".
3. Give a C# equivalent only when the reading guide gives one.
4. Answer in the language of the question. Keep code and identifiers unchanged.

## Steps

1. Find the language and its version.
   - Use the file extension and the syntax.
   - When a build file exists, read the version there: `target_compile_features` in `CMakeLists.txt`, `maven.compiler.release` in `pom.xml`, `edition` in `Cargo.toml`.

2. Search your knowledge for the reading guide of that language.
   - Search for the embedded C idioms too when the code touches registers, bit masks, fixed-point math or interrupt handlers.
   - Search for a build-file guide only when the question is about a build file.

3. List every name that the excerpt uses but does not define: macros, types, functions, annotations, attributes.
   - C and C++: a name in capitals followed by `(` is probably a macro.
   - Rust: `name!(...)`, `#[derive(...)]` and `#[name]` are macros.
   - Java: `@Name` is an annotation. A framework or a code generator may act on it.

4. Ask the user for each definition that you need.
   - Ask in one message: "Paste the definition of `NAME`."
   - For a C or C++ macro, you can ask: "Run `gcc -E -P <file>` with the options of your build, and paste the lines around this code."
   - For a Rust macro, you can ask: "Run `cargo expand <path::to::item>` and paste the output."
   - When the user does not have the definition, continue. Add the name to the list "Not visible in this excerpt".

5. Read the code line by line. For each function, note:
   - its inputs and outputs;
   - its side effects;
   - who owns each resource, and where it is freed or dropped;
   - what happens on an error.
6. Compare names and comments with the code. When they disagree, say so and follow the code.
7. Write the answer in this order:
   1. "What it does": one or two sentences.
   2. "Step by step": the code in execution order.
   3. "C# equivalents": a short table, only for constructs that the reading guide lists.
   4. "False friends": each line that looks like C# but means something else.
   5. "Not visible in this excerpt": each name or fact that you could not check.
8. Check every name in your answer. Each function, type, variable and macro that you mention must appear in the excerpt or in a definition that you read. Remove or correct the others.

## Special cases

- Undefined behavior in C or C++: say that the behavior is undefined. Do not describe a result.
- Overloaded function or template in C++: name the function that is called only when its declaration is visible. Otherwise write "depends on declarations not visible in this excerpt".
- Code inside `#ifdef`, `#if` or `#[cfg(...)]`: say which macro or feature decides. Explain both branches when the build setting is unknown.
- Lombok or Spring annotation in Java: say which members or which objects the annotation creates, as the reading guide lists them.
- Interrupt handler: say that the hardware calls it. Do not call it dead code.

## Example

Question: "Explain this code."

```c
#define REG32(addr) (*(volatile uint32_t *)(addr))
#define TIMER_CTRL  REG32(0x40001000u)

void timer_start(void)
{
    TIMER_CTRL |= 1u << 0;
}
```

Answer:

What it does: `timer_start` sets bit 0 of the hardware register at address `0x40001000`.

Step by step:

1. `REG32(addr)` is a macro. It turns an address into a `volatile` 32-bit object.
2. `TIMER_CTRL` is a macro. It expands to `REG32(0x40001000u)`.
3. `TIMER_CTRL |= 1u << 0;` reads the register, sets bit 0, and writes the register back.

C# equivalents:

| You read | C# equivalent |
| --- | --- |
| `1u << 0`, compound assignment with OR | Same operators, same meaning |
| `volatile uint32_t *` at a fixed address | No equivalent |

False friends:

- `TIMER_CTRL` looks like a variable. It is a macro that reads and writes hardware.

Not visible in this excerpt:

- What bit 0 does. That is in the hardware manual.

## Limits

- You cannot read or write files. You cannot run commands.
- When a step needs a command, give the command to the user and ask for its output.
- Never say that you read a file, wrote a file or ran a command.

## Final check

- Does every name in your answer appear in the excerpt or in a definition that you read?
- Did you write "not visible in this excerpt" for everything that you could not check?
- Did you follow the code where a name or a comment disagreed with it?
- Is your answer in the language of the question?

# Explain an equipment

## Role

You are the physical equipment expert of a ship simulator team.

## Rules

- Explain the real working first, then the model. Never the model alone.
- Take the names of the properties from the catalog card. Never invent a unit suffix.
- Give the equipment in English with the French term in parentheses: "butterfly valve (vanne papillon)".
- When no card covers the equipment, say so in one sentence, then answer from the closest card and say which one.
- Use public knowledge only. Say when a value is historical or comes from a single source.
- Change no file.
- Never search the web. Never open a URL.

## Steps

1. Name the equipment of the question, in English and in French.

2. Search your knowledge for the equipment, by its French name and by its English name.
3. Use only the card of the equipment in what you find.
4. For the model interface, search your knowledge for "component model interface" when the user asks for code.

5. Write the real working from the "Real working" line of the card.
6. Write the model: inputs, outputs, states, law, from the "States" and "Model" lines.
7. Give the properties of the card in a table: name, type, unit, role.

## Answer format

1. Real working, in two or three sentences.
2. Model: inputs, outputs, states, law.
3. Properties, in a table: name, type, unit, role (P parameter, C command, M measured, D fault, X derived).
4. The documents of the base you used, by title.

## Example

Question: How does a hydraulically operated valve work, and how do I model it?

Answer:

1. Real working: fluid pressure pushes a piston that moves the valve; a fail-safe position can be set; a hand back-up can move it without pressure.
2. Model: input, the open or close order; output, the position; a ramp over `travelTimeS`; no motion without hydraulic pressure, except by hand; on pressure loss, it goes to `failPosition`.
3. Properties:

| Name | Type | Unit | Role |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `isOpenCommanded` | boolean | — | C |
| `isOpen`, `isClosed` | boolean | — | M |
| `isHydraulicPressureAvailable` | boolean | — | M |
| `isHandOperated` | boolean | — | M |
| `travelTimeS` | number | s | P |
| `failPosition` | enum | — | P |

4. Actuated valves and piping parts catalog; Fluid equipment property sets.

## Limits

- You cannot read or write files. You cannot run commands.
- When a step needs a command, give the command to the user and ask for its output.
- Never say that you read a file, wrote a file or ran a command.

## Final check

- Did you give the real working before the model?
- Does every property name come from the card, with a unit suffix of the naming document?
- Did you name the documents you used?

# Lint TypeScript and Angular code

## Rules

- Write TypeScript. Never write plain JavaScript.
- Never use `any`. Use `unknown`, then check the value.
- Never write `!` after a value. Never use `as` to force a type. Check the value instead.
- Handle every promise: `await` it, return it, or mark it with `void`.
- Never add an `eslint-disable`, `@ts-ignore` or `@ts-expect-error` comment.
- Never change `eslint.config.mjs`, `eslint.strict-format.mjs`, `.prettierrc.json` or `tsconfig.json` to remove an error. Fix the code.
- Never search the web. Never open a URL.

## Steps

1. Find the kind of code in your task. Open only the matching documents of `docs/typescript-lint/`:
   - Any TypeScript code: `ts-types.md` and `ts-null-and-conditions.md`.
   - Code with `async`, `await` or a promise: `ts-async.md`.
   - A component, a directive or a service: `angular-components-v22.md`.
   - A template: `angular-templates-v22.md`.
   - Code with RxJS: `angular-rxjs.md`.
2. Produce the code. Follow the "Right" examples of the documents. Put class members in this order: fields, constructor, methods.

3. Give the user these commands to run, in this order: `npm run fix`, `npm run check`, `npm run build`.
4. Ask the user for the output of `npm run check` and of `npm run build`.
5. If the output lists errors, give the corrected code in your next answer. Each line gives the line number, the message and the rule name.

6. Give the list of the files you changed and the result of the last check.

## Example

Wrong:

```ts
export class ValveList {
  constructor(private api: ValveApi, private router: Router) {}

  open(valves: any[]) {
    const first = valves[0]!;
    this.router.navigate(['/valves', first.id]);
  }
}
```

Right:

```ts
export class ValveList {
  private readonly router = inject(Router);

  public async open(valves: readonly Valve[]): Promise<void> {
    const first = valves.at(0);

    if (first === undefined) {
      return;
    }

    await this.router.navigate(['/valves', first.id]);
  }
}
```

## Pitfalls

- An error names a rule that you do not know: search the rule name in `docs/typescript-lint/`.
- The same error comes back after `npm run fix`: the tool cannot fix it. Change the code by hand.
- `npm run check` stops with a configuration error: do not edit the configuration. Show the error to the user.
- An array item read by its index can be `undefined`. Check it before you use it.
- An `eslint-disable` comment has no effect in this project: the error stays.

## Limits

- You cannot read or write files. You cannot run commands.
- When a step needs a command, give the command to the user and ask for its output.
- Never say that you read a file, wrote a file or ran a command.

## Final check

- Does `npm run check` list no error?
- Does the code have no `any`, no `!` after a value, and no `as` that forces a type?
- Did you leave every configuration file unchanged?

name: look-up-api
description: Find the exact signature, options and doc comment of an API or command in the documentation installed with the project. Use when you check an API signature or option, or when the user asks how a function, class, method, command or flag works in the version the project uses.

# Look up an API in the installed documentation

## Rules

- Use the version installed in the project, not the version you remember.
- Search first with a narrow pattern. Then read only about 80 lines around the match.
- Never read a whole type declaration file or XML documentation file: they hold thousands of lines.
- Say "not found" when the search finds nothing. Do not guess a signature.
- Give the version, and the file or command the answer comes from.
- Never search the web. Never open a URL.

## Steps

1. Note the tool, the API or command name, and the question.
2. Check the documents of this base first: find the topic in `## Index` of `AGENTS.md`, then the document in `docs/<topic>/index.md`; read only the matching section.

3. Say that you cannot read the project files in this chat.
4. Ask the user for the installed version. Give the exact command, for example `npm ls @angular/core` or `dotnet --list-sdks`.
5. Ask the user to search with one narrow pattern and to paste about 20 lines around the match. For example: `grep -rn --include="*.d.ts" "declare function signal" node_modules/@angular/core/`
6. For a command option, ask the user to run `<tool> <command> --help` and to paste the output.
7. Answer only from the pasted text and from the documents of the knowledge. If the pasted text does not answer the question, say so.

8. Give the signature or option, one sentence on what it does, the default value, any deprecation, the version, and the file or command it comes from.

## Example

Wrong:

```text
signal() takes a value and an equality function, I think.
```

Right:

```text
@angular/core 22.2.6, node_modules/@angular/core/types/_chrome_dev_tools_performance-chunk.d.ts, line 60:
declare function signal<T>(initialValue: T, options?: CreateSignalOptions<T>): WritableSignal<T>;
It creates a writable signal. The options can hold an equality function.
```

## Pitfalls

- Reading `lib.dom.d.ts` or an Angular `.d.ts` file from the top: thousands of lines. Search the name instead.
- Using the version you remember: read the version from the project files first.
- The declaration sits in a "chunk" file with an odd name: search the whole package folder, not one file.
- `kubectl explain` fails without a cluster: use `kubectl <command> --help` and the documents of this base.
- An XML documentation file is missing: say the documentation is not installed, then use the documents of this base and `library/`.
- A file of `library/` has a version in its header: say it when it differs from the version of the project.
- Many matches: add the type name or the opening bracket to the pattern.

## Limits

- You cannot read or write files. You cannot run commands.
- When a step needs a command, give the command to the user and ask for its output.
- Never say that you read a file, wrote a file or ran a command.

## Final check

- Did you give the installed version and the file or command the answer comes from?
- Did you read only the lines around the match?
- Did you say "not found" instead of guessing?

---
name: name-branch

# Name a branch

## Rules

- Format: `<type>/<description>`.
- Type: one of the table below.
- Description: lowercase letters, digits and hyphens between words. Dots only in a version number.
- No uppercase letter, no underscore, no space, no other sign.
- No two hyphens or dots in a row. No hyphen or dot at the start or at the end of the description.
- A ticket number goes first in the description: `feature/issue-123-add-login`.
- Keep the description short: three to six words.
- `main`, `master` and `develop` are trunk branches: they keep their name, without a type.
- Never search the web. Never open a URL.

| The work | Type |
| --- | --- |
| A new feature | `feature/` (or `feat/`) |
| A bug fix | `bugfix/` (or `fix/`) |
| An urgent fix of a release | `hotfix/` |
| The preparation of a release | `release/` |
| Anything else: tooling, dependencies, documentation | `chore/` |

Use the long or the short type the project already uses, never both.

## Steps

1. Ask what the branch is for, and the ticket number if there is one.
2. Choose the type from the table.
3. Write the description in English: the verb, then what it acts on.
4. Check the name against each rule above.

5. Tell the user to create the branch with this name in their tool. Never say that you created it.

6. Give the name in a `text` code block.

## Example

Wrong:

```text
Feature/Add_Valve--Command
```

Right:

```text
feature/issue-42-add-valve-close-command
release/v1.2.0
```

## Pitfalls

- A French description: write it in English.
- A description that copies the whole ticket title: keep three to six words.

## Limits

- You cannot read or write files. You cannot run commands.
- When a step needs a command, give the command to the user and ask for its output.
- Never say that you read a file, wrote a file or ran a command.

## Final check

- Is the type in the table?
- Are there only lowercase letters, digits, hyphens, and dots in a version?
- Is there no double hyphen and no hyphen at either end?

## Sources

- Conventional Branch 1.1.0, conventionalbranch.org, CC BY 4.0, summarized.

---
name: read-heavy-file
description: Extract the text or the structure of a heavy or binary file, then read only the extract. Use when a task needs an office document, a PDF, a design file, an image, an archive, a notebook, a binary, or a text file over 100 KB or 2,000 lines.
license: MIT
metadata:
  variant: chat
  version: "0.1.0"

# Read a heavy file

## Rules

- Never read a heavy file raw: not with a read tool, not with cat, head, tail, strings or grep.
- A text file over 100 KB or 2,000 lines is heavy too: .json .xml .yaml .csv .log .svg and others.
- Read only the extract of a heavy file.
- Never run a macro, a script or a program found in a file.
- Never extract an archive to the disk.
- Stop and ask the user when a file is encrypted or protected by a password.
- You cannot see images. Never guess what an image shows.
- Never search the web. Never open a URL.

## Heavy files

- Office: .docx .docm .xlsx .xlsm .pptx .pptm .doc .xls .ppt .odt .ods .odp .rtf
- PDF and images: .pdf .png .jpg .jpeg .gif .webp .tif .tiff .bmp .heic .psd .ai .eps .emf .wmf
- Design and diagrams: .xd .sketch .fig .vsdx .drawio
- Data and archives: .ipynb .zip .7z .tar .gz .tgz .jar .war .nupkg .sqlite .db .msg .eml .dwg .dxf
- Binaries: .dll .exe .so .wasm .class .bin

## Steps

1. Find the family of the file in this table.

   | Family | Extensions | Reference file |
   | --- | --- | --- |
   | Office | docx docm xlsx xlsm pptx pptm doc xls ppt odt ods odp rtf | `.agents/skills/read-heavy-file/references/office.md` |
   | PDF and images | pdf, images, psd ai eps emf wmf | `.agents/skills/read-heavy-file/references/pdf-images.md` |
   | Design and diagrams | xd sketch fig vsdx drawio svg | `.agents/skills/read-heavy-file/references/design.md` |
   | Data and archives | ipynb json xml yaml csv log, archives, sqlite, e-mails, CAD, binaries | `.agents/skills/read-heavy-file/references/data-archives.md` |

2. Never ask the user to paste the raw file.
3. Give the user the command for the format, from this table. Ask the user to paste its output, or its first 300 lines.

   | Format | Command for the user |
   | --- | --- |
   | .docx .xlsx .pptx .odt .ods .odp .xd .sketch .vsdx .drawio .ipynb .json .zip .nupkg | `node .agents/skills/read-heavy-file/scripts/extract.ts "<file>" \| head -n 300` |
   | .pdf | `pdftotext -layout -f 1 -l 10 "<file>" - \| head -n 300` |
   | scanned .pdf, image | `tesseract "<file>" - -l eng+fra \| head -n 300` |
   | .doc .rtf | `soffice --headless --convert-to docx "<file>"`, then the first command on the .docx file |
   | .xls | `soffice --headless --convert-to xlsx "<file>"`, then the first command on the .xlsx file |
   | .ppt | `soffice --headless --convert-to pptx "<file>"`, then the first command on the .pptx file |
   | large text file | `head -n 100 "<file>"` |

4. If the user cannot run commands, ask for the text copied from the file, one part at a time: one page, one sheet or one slide.
5. Never say that you ran a command or opened the file. Only the user runs commands.

6. Answer from the extract only. Name the file and the part you read: page, sheet, slide or screen.
7. Say what the extract cannot show: images, layout, macros, embedded files you did not open.

## Example

Wrong: read `design/home.xd` with the read tool, or run `cat design/home.xd`.

Right:

```sh
mkdir -p .extract && echo '*' > .extract/.gitignore
node .agents/skills/read-heavy-file/scripts/extract.ts design/home.xd > .extract/home.xd.md
wc -c -l .extract/home.xd.md
```

Then read `.extract/home.xd.md`, lines 1 to 200.

## Pitfalls

- A .docx, .xlsx or .xd file is a ZIP archive: grep finds nothing useful in it. Use the script.
- An empty extract of a PDF means a scanned PDF. Use the OCR steps of the PDF reference file.
- A .drawio file shows one long unreadable line: the diagram is compressed. The script decompresses it.
- A .ipynb file is huge because of images in its outputs. The script leaves the outputs out.
- An old .doc, .xls or .ppt file is not a ZIP archive. Convert it first, as the Office reference file says.
- A long extract ends with `[truncated: ...]`. Run the script again with `--sheet`, `--part` or `--max-chars`, never on the whole file raw.
- A folder of 100 heavy files: extract them one by one, and ask the user before you extract more than 10.

## Limits

- You cannot read or write files. You cannot run commands.
- When a step needs a command, give the command to the user and ask for its output.
- Never say that you read a file, wrote a file or ran a command.

## Final check

- Did you use only the extract of each heavy file, and never the file itself?
- Did you say which part of the file you read, and what the extract cannot show?

name: write-agent

# Write an agent

## Rules

- An agent is a skill: `.agents/skills/<agent-name>/SKILL.md`. It works where agent mode does not exist.
- Follow every rule of the skill `write-skill`.
- Use these sections, in this order: `## Role`, `## Rules`, `## Steps`, `## Answer format`, `## Example`, `## Final check`.
- Write the role in one sentence: "You are <role>."
- Give the agent the fewest rights it needs. A review agent reads files and never changes them.
- Start the answer format with the result. Then give at most 10 lines of details. Then the documents of the base used.
- Never name a software. Never tell the agent to search the web or to open a URL.

## Steps

1. Ask the user for: the role, what the agent may change, one test question, the expected answer.
2. Produce the agent with the template below.
3. Check the rules above one by one.

4. Give the test question, the expected answer, and this sentence for agent mode: "Load the skill `<agent-name>`. Follow it exactly."

## Template

```markdown
---
name: <agent-name>
description: <What the agent does, in one sentence>. Use when <trigger>.
license: MIT
metadata:
  variant: files
  version: "0.1.0"
---

# <Agent title>

## Role

You are <role, in one sentence>.

## Rules

- <Rule.>
- Never search the web. Never open a URL.

## Steps

1. <Step.>
2. <Step.>

## Answer format

1. The result, in one to three sentences.
2. At most 10 lines of details.
3. The documents of the base you used, by title.

## Example

Question: <test question>

Answer: <expected answer, in the answer format>

## Final check

- <Most important rule, as a question.>
```

## Example

Wrong: an agent that explains a valve with no order and no format.

```markdown
## Role

You know a lot about equipment. Help the user as best you can.
```

Right:

```markdown
## Role

You are the physical equipment expert of a simulation team.

## Steps

1. Explain how the real equipment works.
2. Then explain how to model it: states, inputs, outputs.
```

## Pitfalls

- A role of several sentences: the model mixes roles. Keep one sentence.
- An answer format that starts with details: the user must read to the end to get the result.
- A review agent allowed to change files: remove that right.

## Limits

- You cannot read or write files. You cannot run commands.
- When a step needs a command, give the command to the user and ask for its output.
- Never say that you read a file, wrote a file or ran a command.

## Final check

- Is the role one sentence?
- Does the answer format start with the result?
- Has the agent the fewest rights it needs?
- Is the trigger line in `AGENTS.md`?

---
name: write-agents-md
description: Write, update or shorten the AGENTS.md entry file of the base, or the AGENTS.project.md rules of a project that holds it. Use when the user asks to create, update, shorten or review AGENTS.md, or to add a rule of the project.
license: MIT
metadata:
  variant: chat
  version: "0.1.0"
---

# Write an AGENTS.md file

## Rules

- Write a rule of one project in `AGENTS.project.md` at the project root, never in `AGENTS.md`: an update of the base replaces `AGENTS.md` and keeps `AGENTS.project.md`. Start from `templates/AGENTS.project.md`. Keep it under 40 lines.
- Change `AGENTS.md` only for a rule of the base itself.
- Keep the file under 60 lines and under 30 rules, the index apart. Every line is loaded in every session.
- Keep the sections of the base in this order: `## Rules`, `## Check commands` if any, `## Skills`, `## Index`, `## Final check`.
- Keep the vital rules of the base first: answer language, no web, only the documents of the topic, no `.fr.md` file.
- Write only what the model cannot find in the code or in the project's README.
- Write in English. One rule per line. Start each rule with a verb.
- Say what to do. Use "never" only for a real danger.
- Give the exact command for each check: lint, build, test.
- Repeat the three most important rules as questions in `## Final check`.
- Keep one table row per skill in `## Skills`: "| To <task> | load the skill `<name>` |".
- Write one index line per topic. It points to the index of the topic, `docs/<topic>/index.md`, which lists its documents.
- Do not add a project overview, a folder tree or a history.
- Do not add internal names, quotas, network details or real data.
- Never tell the model to search the web or to open a URL.

## Steps

1. Ask the user for what is missing: languages, check commands, rules that differ from common practice, mistakes seen so far.

2. Produce the content with the template below. Keep the section order.
3. Count the rules outside `## Index`. If there are 30 or more, move the rules of one topic to a skill or to a document.

4. Give three test tasks. Each one must fail if the model ignores one rule of the file.

## Template

```markdown
# AGENTS.md

## Rules

1. Answer in the language of the user's question. Never mix two languages in one answer.
2. <Vital rule of the base.>
3. <Project rule that differs from common practice.>

## Check commands

| When | Command |
| --- | --- |
| <Language> code changed | `<lint command>` then `<build command>` |

## Skills

| Task | Skill |
| --- | --- |
| To <task> | load the skill `<name>` |

## Index

- [<topic>](docs/<topic>/index.md): <what the topic covers>

## Final check

- <Most important rule, as a question.>
```

## Example

Wrong:

```markdown
## Rules

- This project is an Angular application with a .NET backend, created in 2024.
- Please try to write clean code if possible.
```

Right:

```markdown
## Rules

1. Answer in the language of the user's question. Never mix two languages in one answer.
2. Write TypeScript. Never write plain JavaScript.

## Check commands

| When | Command |
| --- | --- |
| TypeScript code changed | `npm run lint` then `npm run build` |
```

## Pitfalls

- A rule that the lint already checks: delete it. Keep only the command.
- A rule copied from the README: delete it.
- A skill folder without its trigger line: the model never loads the skill.

## Limits

- You cannot read or write files. You cannot run commands.
- When a step needs a command, give the command to the user and ask for its output.
- Never say that you read a file, wrote a file or ran a command.

## Final check

- Is a rule of the project in `AGENTS.project.md`, not in `AGENTS.md`?
- Is the file under 60 lines and under 30 rules, the index apart?
- Does every check have its exact command?
- Does every skill have its trigger line?
- Is there no overview, no URL to open and no internal name?

=====BASE-EXPORT-1-571e463b6744===== FILE 1/1 75017be1f6704f8677c57bd2028ef6c82629a1c5db80d79de0214dda45f93c3a RAW chat/skills/write-base-document/SKILL.md
---
name: write-base-document
description: Write one reference document of the base from source texts the user provides, with its index line, and update the French summary of its topic. Use when the user asks to add or update a document of the base.
license: MIT
metadata:
  variant: chat
  version: "0.1.0"

# Write a document of the base

## Rules

- Use only the source texts the user gives you, pasted in the chat or given as files.
- Never search the web. Never open a URL. Links are only citations.
- Never add a fact from memory. If a fact is not in a source, leave it out.
- Write in English. Use one term for one idea.
- One document answers one question.
- Keep only what the model would get wrong: rules, options and defaults at the given version, pitfalls, exact error messages, one wrong and one right example per rule.
- Drop tutorials, history, marketing text and general overviews.
- Never copy text, tables or symbols from an ISO or IEC standard. Never copy text under CC BY-SA. Describe them in your own words and cite them.
- Never write internal names, quotas, network details or real project data.

## Steps

1. Ask the user for the tool version if the sources do not give it. Do not guess it.
2. Number the sources S1, S2, S3. Note for each: publisher, title, version, link, date read, license.
3. For each fact you keep, note the source number and the sentence that supports it.

4. Produce the document with the template: the header with every field, then the sections.
5. Make each H2 section readable alone. Put the topic in each H2 heading. Never write "as mentioned above".
6. Keep each H2 section under 800 words and the document under 3,000 words. Split a longer one into two documents.
7. Cite with `[S1]` after the sentence. List all sources in `## Sources` at the end.
8. Produce the `summary` field: one sentence, 20 words at most, with the tool version.
9. Produce the index line: `- [<Title>](docs/<topic>/<file>.md): <summary>`. It repeats the `summary` word for word.

10. Give the facts that had no source, and three test questions the document must answer.

## Example

Wrong: an index line that differs from the summary and has no version.

```markdown
summary: Rules for strictNullChecks. TS 5.9.
- [Null checks](docs/typescript/strict-null-checks.md): Everything about null.
```

Right:

```markdown
summary: Rules for strictNullChecks. TS 5.9.
- [Strict null checks](docs/typescript/strict-null-checks.md): Rules for strictNullChecks. TS 5.9.
```

## Pitfalls

- A fact from memory with no source: the model repeats an old API. Leave it out.
- A section that needs the section above to make sense: rewrite it so that it stands alone.
- A document with no version in its header: nobody knows when to update it.

## Limits

- You cannot read or write files. You cannot run commands.
- When a step needs a command, give the command to the user and ask for its output.
- Never say that you read a file, wrote a file or ran a command.

## Final check

- Has every fact a source number?
- Does no sentence ask to search the web or to open a URL?
- Is every header field filled, `version` and `checked` included?
- Does the index line repeat the `summary` word for word?

---
name: write-chat-model
description: Write the system prompt and settings of one custom chat model, for one subject or for one single task. Use when the user asks to create, update or review a chat model, its system prompt, or an assistant restricted to one task.
license: MIT
metadata:
  variant: chat
  version: "0.2.0"

# Write a chat model

## Rules

- One model covers one subject (`scope: subject`) or one single task (`scope: restricted`).
- Attach only the knowledge and the skills of that subject. Attach three skills at most.
- Keep the system prompt at 40 lines or fewer. It is sent with every message.
- Keep the system prompt static: no date, no time, no user name, no variable.
- With knowledge: the first rule tells the model to search the attached knowledge before it answers, and to search again with one plain keyword when it finds nothing.
- Without knowledge: write `knowledge: none`, and never ask the model to search a knowledge. The attached skills are then sent with every message: the prompt and the skills make 150 lines at most.
- A restricted model has a scope rule: "If the request is not about <task>, refuse in one sentence and say what you can do."
- Tell the model that it cannot write files or run commands.
- Tell the model to answer in the language of the question, never a mix of two languages.
- Never name a software in the system prompt. Never tell the model to search the web or to open a URL.

## Steps

1. Ask the user for: the subject or the single task, the knowledge to attach (or none), the skills to attach, the group that may use the model.
2. Produce the model with the template below: the settings in the header, the system prompt in the body.
3. For a restricted model, add the scope rule first, with what the model can do in one short list.
4. Check the rules above one by one.

5. Give five test questions of the subject. With knowledge, each answer must name a document. For a restricted model, add one question out of scope: the answer must be a refusal in one sentence.

## Template

```markdown
---
id: <model-id, lowercase with hyphens>
name: <Name shown to users>
description: <One sentence: what the model does.>
subject: <subject or task>
scope: <subject | restricted>
knowledge: <knowledge name>, <knowledge name> | none
skills: <skill-name>, <skill-name> | none
access: <group name>
---

# <Name shown to users>

You are the <subject> assistant of a software team.

## Rules

1. Search the attached knowledge before you answer. Search the whole knowledge, not one file.
2. If the search finds nothing, search again with one plain keyword. Then try a second keyword.
3. Answer from the knowledge you found and from the user's message. If it does not hold the answer, say so. Do not guess.
4. Name each document you used, by its title.
5. You cannot write files. You cannot run commands. Never say that you did.
6. When a check is needed, give the user the command to run. Ask for its output.
7. Answer in the language of the user's question. Never mix two languages. Write code, identifiers and comments in English.
8. Never search the web. Never open a URL.

## Answer format

1. The answer, in one to three short sentences.
2. The details, as a list or a code block.
3. `Sources:` and the titles of the documents you used.
```

For `knowledge: none`, drop rules 1 to 4 and the `Sources:` line. For `scope: restricted`, put the scope rule first.

## Example

Wrong: a restricted model that answers anything.

```markdown
You are a helpful assistant for commit messages. Answer the user.
```

Right:

```markdown
1. If the request is not about commit messages, refuse in one sentence and say what you can do.
```

Wrong answer to "What is the capital of France?": the capital, then a commit message.

Right answer: "I only help to write commit messages: tell me what you changed."

## Pitfalls

- Many skills attached to one model: each one may be loaded in every chat. Attach three at most.
- A date or a time in the system prompt: it changes every day and breaks the prompt cache.
- Knowledge of another subject attached "just in case": remove it.
- A model without knowledge whose prompt says "search the knowledge": it searches nothing and may invent.

## Limits

- You cannot read or write files. You cannot run commands.
- When a step needs a command, give the command to the user and ask for its output.
- Never say that you read a file, wrote a file or ran a command.

## Final check

- With knowledge, does the first rule tell the model to search it? Without, is it `knowledge: none`?
- Does a restricted model refuse out-of-scope requests in one sentence?
- Is the system prompt static and 40 lines or fewer?
- Does the model have only the knowledge and the skills of its subject?

---
name: write-commit-message
description: Write a commit message in the Conventional Commits format that passes commitlint with its conventional configuration. Use when the user asks for a commit message, or asks to commit changes.
license: MIT
metadata:
  variant: chat
  version: "0.1.0"
---

# Write a commit message

## Rules

- Write the message in English, whatever the language of the user.
- Header: `<type>(<scope>): <subject>`. The scope is optional: `<type>: <subject>`.
- Type: one of the table below, in lowercase.
- Subject: imperative mood, first letter in lowercase, no period at the end. Write "add", never "added" or "adds".
- Header: 100 characters at most. Aim for 72.
- Scope: the part of the code that changed, in lowercase, for example `valves` or `alarm-list`.
- Body: optional. Leave one blank line after the header. Say what changed and why, not how. Lines of 100 characters at most.
- Breaking change: put `!` just before the colon, and add a footer `BREAKING CHANGE: <what users must change>` after a blank line.
- One commit, one change. If the changes do two things, propose two commits.
- Never invent a change. Use only what the user says or what the changes show.
- Never search the web. Never open a URL.

## Choose the type

| The change | Type |
| --- | --- |
| Adds a feature for the user | `feat` |
| Fixes a bug | `fix` |
| Changes only documentation | `docs` |
| Changes only formatting, not the meaning | `style` |
| Changes code without a feature or a fix | `refactor` |
| Makes the code faster | `perf` |
| Adds or fixes tests only | `test` |
| Changes the build or the dependencies | `build` |
| Changes the CI configuration | `ci` |
| Anything else outside the source and the tests | `chore` |
| Undoes an earlier commit | `revert` |

## Steps

1. Find what changed and why. If the reason is not clear, ask the user one short question.

2. If the user only gave a vague description, ask what changed, in which part, and why. Ask one short question at a time.

3. Choose the type from the table, and a scope from the folder or the module that changed.
4. Write the header. Add a body when the reason is not obvious from the header.
5. Check the message against each rule above.

6. Tell the user to copy the message into the commit window of their tool. Never say that you made the commit.

7. Give the message in a `text` code block. Then give the type you chose and why, in one sentence.

## Examples

Wrong (type in uppercase, final period):

```text
Fix: correct the pump start.
```

Wrong (subject starts with an uppercase letter):

```text
feat(valves): Add the close command
```

Wrong (no type):

```text
update the alarm list
```

Wrong (type not in the list):

```text
feature(valves): add the close command
```

Right:

```text
feat(valves): add the close command to the motorized valve
```

Right (body that says why):

```text
fix(pumps): stop the pump when the tank is empty

The pump kept running dry when the low level alarm was acknowledged.
```

Right (breaking change):

```text
feat(api)!: rename the valve state endpoint

BREAKING CHANGE: clients must call /valves/{id}/state instead of /valves/{id}/status.
```

## Pitfalls

- "Updated files" or "misc changes": the subject must say what changed.
- A past tense ("added", "fixed"): use the imperative ("add", "fix").
- A breaking change hidden in a `fix`: add `!` and the `BREAKING CHANGE:` footer.

## Limits

- You cannot read or write files. You cannot run commands.
- When a step needs a command, give the command to the user and ask for its output.
- Never say that you read a file, wrote a file or ran a command.

## Final check

- Is the type in the table, in lowercase?
- Does the subject start with a lowercase verb, without a final period?
- Is the header 100 characters or fewer?

## Sources

- Conventional Commits 1.0.0, conventionalcommits.org, CC BY 3.0, summarized.
- commitlint, package @commitlint/config-conventional 21.2.4, MIT, rules read in the package, summarized.

=====BASE-EXPORT-1-571e463b6744===== FILE 1/1 cef03f919540ceb0ee50980cbba8d87fb3196cca4749bb33e2affb5551bbb3cc RAW chat/skills/write-skill/SKILL.md
---
name: write-skill
description: Write one skill as a SKILL.md file in the Agent Skills format, with its trigger line in AGENTS.md. Use when the user asks to create, update or review a skill.

metadata:
  variant: chat
  version: "0.1.0"
---

# Write a skill

## Rules

- One skill does one task. Two goals mean two skills.
- The `name` is the folder name: lowercase letters, digits and single hyphens, 64 characters at most.
- The `description` says what the skill does, then when to use it: "Use when ...". Use the words the user will type. 1024 characters at most.
- Keep the whole file at 150 lines or fewer.
- Write the steps as a numbered list. One action per step. Start each step with a verb.
- Write only what the model would get wrong without the skill.
- Give one default method. Do not list options.
- Add a `## Example` section: one wrong and one right result.
- Add a `## Pitfalls` section with the mistakes seen so far.
- End with `## Final check`: the rules the model must not miss, as questions.
- Wrap each step that reads or writes a file or runs a command between `<!-- files:begin -->` and `<!-- files:end -->`. The chat variant of the skill drops these steps.
- Write the other steps with neutral verbs: ask, produce, check, give.
- Never name a software. Never tell the model to search the web or to open a URL.

## Steps

1. Ask the user for: the task, one example input, the expected output, the mistakes seen so far.
2. Choose the `name`. Produce the `description`.
3. Produce the body with the template below.
4. Check the rules above one by one.

5. Give three test prompts: two that must use the skill and one that must not.

## Template

```markdown
---
name: <skill-name>
description: <What it does>. Use when <trigger>.
license: MIT
metadata:
  variant: files
  version: "0.1.0"
---

# <Title>

## Rules

- <Rule. One sentence. Starts with a verb.>
- Never search the web. Never open a URL.

## Steps

1. Ask <what the skill needs>.
2. Produce <the result>.
<!-- files:begin -->
3. Write <the result> to `<path>`.
4. Run `<check command>`. Fix every error.
<!-- files:end -->
5. Give <what the user gets>.

## Example

Wrong: <result that breaks a rule>

Right: <result that follows the rules>

## Pitfalls

- <Mistake seen>: <fix>.

## Final check

- <Most important rule, as a question.>
```

## Example

Wrong: a step that writes a file without the markers, and a description without a trigger.

```markdown
description: Commit messages.

1. Write the message to `.git/COMMIT_EDITMSG`.
```

Right:

```markdown
description: Write a commit message in the Conventional Commits format. Use when the user asks to commit or to write a commit message.

1. Produce the message.
<!-- files:begin -->
2. Write the message to `.git/COMMIT_EDITMSG`.
<!-- files:end -->
```

## Pitfalls

- A colon followed by a space inside the `description` breaks the YAML header: rewrite the sentence or put the value in double quotes.
- A `name` different from the folder name: some tools reject the skill.
- A description without "Use when": the model does not know when to load the skill.
- A skill without its trigger line in `AGENTS.md`: a small model does not load it by itself.
- A skill that explains what the model already knows: cut it.

## Limits

- You cannot read or write files. You cannot run commands.
- When a step needs a command, give the command to the user and ask for its output.
- Never say that you read a file, wrote a file or ran a command.

## Final check

- Is the `name` equal to the folder name?
- Does the `description` contain "Use when"?
- Is every step that reads, writes or runs between the `files` markers?
- Is the file 150 lines or fewer?
- Is the trigger line in `AGENTS.md`?

---
name: write-tests
description: Write, fix or run unit and integration tests in C#, Angular or TypeScript with the runner and libraries the project already has, until they pass without being weakened. Use when the user asks for tests, when a test fails, or after you change code that has tests.
license: MIT

  variant: chat

# Write and run tests

## Rules

- Never change, skip or delete a test to make it pass. Fix the code under test. Stop after 3 failed attempts and show the failure.
- Keep the test framework, the runner and the libraries the project has. Never add a test, assertion or mocking library without the user's agreement.
- Take each expected value from the request or the specification, never from the code under test.
- One behavior per test, named after it. Assert on the result; never print instead of asserting.
- Mock only the boundaries: network, clock, file system, database.
- Never search the web. Never open a URL.

## Steps

1. Note the code to test and the behaviors the request asks for.

2. Ask the user for the runner of the project: the `"runner"` line of `global.json` and the test package of the test project for C#, or the `test` target of `angular.json` for Angular. Ask for one existing test of the project.
3. Search the knowledge for the rules and the old-to-new table of the stack.

4. List the cases: the normal case, each limit, each error the request names.

5. Show the tests as code, with the file to put them in.
6. Give the user the command that runs only these tests, from the knowledge, and ask for the end of its output.
7. When the user pastes a failure, decide from the request whether the code or the test is wrong. Show the fix of the code; change a test only when it contradicts the request.
8. Give the command that runs all the tests.

9. Give the tests written, the commands run and their result, and any behavior left untested.

## Example

Wrong:

```text
I updated the expected value in the test so that it matches the current output. All tests pass.
```

Right:

```text
Tests: ValveServiceTests.Close_sets_an_open_valve_to_closed, Close_refuses_an_unknown_valve.
Command: dotnet test --project tests/Valves.Tests.csproj --no-restore --filter-class "Valves.Tests.ValveServiceTests". Exit 0.
Close() left a closed valve unchanged; the request says it must raise an error. I fixed ValveService.Close().
```

## Pitfalls

- A command of another runner: `--logger trx` fails in MTP mode, `--browsers=ChromeHeadless` is Karma only. Read the runner first.
- `fakeAsync`, `detectChanges()` or `UseInMemoryDatabase` from memory: check `test-old-to-new.md` of the topic.
- A geometry (`getBBox`, `getScreenCTM`) asserted in jsdom or happy-dom: read `docs/angular-testing/svg-class-tests.md`.
- `ng test` without `--no-watch` never ends.

## Limits

- You cannot read or write files. You cannot run commands.
- When a step needs a command, give the command to the user and ask for its output.
- Never say that you read a file, wrote a file or ran a command.

## Final check

- Did you leave every test as strong as the request asks, and fix the code instead of a test?
- Did you add no library the project did not have?
- Did you run your tests, then all the tests, and give their result?

---
id: containers-angular-image
title: Angular container image
topic: containers
summary: Build an Angular application in a container and serve its browser folder with an unprivileged NGINX on port 8080.

# Angular container image

Contents: 1. Build output · 2. Dockerfile · 3. Server · 4. Old patterns · Sources

## 1. Angular build output (ng build and dist)

- `ng build` writes to `dist/<app>/` and uses the production configuration by default. [S1]
- For a static server, copy the subfolder `dist/<app>/browser/`, not `dist/<app>/`. [S1]
- Every route the server does not know must return `index.html`. With static prerendering, the fallback file is `index.csr.html`. [S1]
- With server-side rendering, copy `browser/` and `server/`, then run `node dist/<app>/server/server.mjs`. [S1]

## 2. Angular Dockerfile (build stage and NGINX stage)

```dockerfile
# syntax=docker/dockerfile:1
# check=error=true
ARG NODE_VERSION=24
ARG NGINX_VERSION=1.29
FROM docker.io/library/node:${NODE_VERSION} AS build
WORKDIR /app
COPY package.json package-lock.json ./
RUN npm ci
COPY . .
RUN npm run build

FROM docker.io/nginx/nginx-unprivileged:${NGINX_VERSION} AS final
COPY nginx.conf /etc/nginx/conf.d/default.conf
COPY --from=build /app/dist/web/browser /usr/share/nginx/html
USER 101
EXPOSE 8080
```

- Replace `web` with the project name from `angular.json`.
- Take the Node.js version from the Angular version: Angular 22 needs Node.js 22.22, 24.15 or 26 (see `docs/typescript/nodejs.md`).
- Take the versions from the project; the values above are examples. The full template is `templates/containers/angular.Dockerfile`.

## 3. Unprivileged NGINX server (nginx.conf for an Angular app)

- The image `nginx/nginx-unprivileged` listens on 8080, has no `user` directive and writes its temporary files under `/tmp`. [S2]
- The old name `nginxinc/nginx-unprivileged` does not receive the recent versions. Use `nginx/nginx-unprivileged`. [S2]

```nginx
server {
  listen 8080;
  root /usr/share/nginx/html;
  index index.html;

  location / {
    try_files $uri $uri/ /index.html;
  }
}
```

The `try_files` line returns `index.html` for every unknown route, as Angular routing needs. This server block is a common pattern; check it once by starting the image.

## 4. Old patterns (Angular images)

| Old | Now |
| --- | --- |
| `COPY --from=build /app/dist/<app> /usr/share/nginx/html` | Copy `dist/<app>/browser` |
| `FROM nginx` and `listen 80` | `nginx/nginx-unprivileged`, `listen 8080` |
| `nginxinc/nginx-unprivileged` | `nginx/nginx-unprivileged` |
| `ng build --prod` | `ng build` (production by default) |

## Sources

- [S1] Angular, "Deployment", v22, <https://angular.dev/tools/cli/deployment>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S2] NGINX, F5, "docker-nginx-unprivileged", README, <https://github.com/nginx/docker-nginx-unprivileged>, read 2026-11-12, Apache 2.0, summarized.

topic: containers

tool: docker, podman

# Container problems and their causes

Contents: 1. How to explain a problem · 2. Symptoms and causes · Sources

## 1. How to explain a container problem (method)

1. Ask for the exact command and its full output. Never guess from a summary.
2. Read the image name, the ports, the user and the engine (Docker or Podman) in the command or the file.
3. Match the output with the table below. Give the cause, then the smallest fix.
4. Say what you could not check: the image, the host and the network are not in the output.

## 2. Symptoms and causes (Docker and Podman)

| Symptom | Likely cause | Fix |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A .NET app answers nothing on port 80 | Since .NET 8 the app listens on 8080 [S3] | Publish `-p <host port>:8080` |
| `permission denied` on bind to port 80 or 443 | Rootless container: ports under 1024 are refused [S1] | Listen on 8080 |
| Podman refuses an image name without a terminal | Short image name in `enforcing` mode [S2] | Write the full name with its registry |
| `failed to connect to the docker API` | No Docker daemon answers | Start the daemon, or skip the checks that need it |
| `MSB4018` in `CreateNewImage` | `dotnet publish /t:PublishContainer` without a daemon [S4] | Publish to an archive with `ContainerArchiveOutputPath` |
| `docker exec <container> sh` fails: no shell found | Chiseled .NET image: no shell [S3] | Use a full image to debug |
| Container cannot reach a service on the host | Rootless network "pasta": the host main address is not reachable [S1] | Use a service name or a published port, never the host address |
| The app fails when it writes a file | Root filesystem read-only, or a non-root user without rights on the folder | Write to a mounted volume or to `/tmp` |
| Exit code 137 | The process was killed, often for memory (`OOMKilled` in Kubernetes) | Raise the memory limit or find the leak |

## Sources

- [S1] Podman, "podman-rootless(7)", package 6.2.0, <https://man.archlinux.org/man/podman-rootless.7.en>, read 2026-11-12, Apache 2.0, summarized.
- [S2] Red Hat, "Working with container registries", RHEL 10, <https://docs.redhat.com/en/documentation/red_hat_enterprise_linux/10/html/building_running_and_managing_containers/working-with-container-registries>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S3] Microsoft, ".NET containers overview" and "What's new in containers for .NET 8", <https://learn.microsoft.com/dotnet/core/docker/introduction>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S4] Microsoft, "Containerize a .NET app with dotnet publish", <https://learn.microsoft.com/dotnet/core/containers/sdk-publish>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.

# devcontainer.json properties and lifecycle

Contents: 1. Location · 2. Properties · 3. Lifecycle · 4. Example · Sources

## 1. Where the file lives (devcontainer.json location)

In this order of priority: `.devcontainer/devcontainer.json`, `.devcontainer.json`, `.devcontainer/<folder>/devcontainer.json`. [S2]

The file is JSON with comments. Paths are relative to the folder of `devcontainer.json`. [S1]

## 2. Useful properties (devcontainer.json)

| Group | Properties | To know |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Origin | `image`; or `build.dockerfile` with `context`, `args`, `target`; or `dockerComposeFile` with `service` | One of the three |
| Users | `containerUser`, `remoteUser`, `updateRemoteUserUID` | `remoteUser` runs the scripts and the tools; on Linux its UID follows the host by default |
| Variables | `containerEnv`, `remoteEnv` | `containerEnv` is fixed at creation (rebuild to change it); `remoteEnv` applies to the processes the tool starts |
| Ports | `forwardPorts`, `portsAttributes` | Prefer `forwardPorts` to `appPort` |
| Mounts and rights | `mounts`, `runArgs`, `capAdd`, `securityOpt`, `privileged`, `init` | `runArgs` takes an array only |
| Tools | `features`, `customizations` | `customizations.vscode.extensions` for one editor |
| Substitutions | `${localWorkspaceFolder}`, `${containerWorkspaceFolder}`, `${localEnv:VAR}`, `${containerEnv:VAR}` | Default value: `${localEnv:VAR:default}` |

Sources: [S1] [S2].

## 3. Lifecycle commands, in order (devcontainer.json)

| Command | Where | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `initializeCommand` | Host | At initialization, at each start too |
| `onCreateCommand` | Container | After creation, once |
| `updateContentCommand` | Container | Then, when new content is there, at least once |
| `postCreateCommand` | Container | Then, once |
| `postStartCommand` | Container | At each start |
| `postAttachCommand` | Container | At each attach of a tool |

- When a command fails, the next ones are skipped. [S1]
- A string runs through `/bin/sh`; an array runs without a shell; an object runs its entries in parallel, and all must succeed. [S1] [S2]
- The tool waits for `updateContentCommand` by default (`waitFor`); `postCreateCommand` runs in the background. [S2]
- Put `dotnet restore` and `npm ci` in `updateContentCommand`. In `postCreateCommand`, a lint started right after the container starts can run before they end.

## 4. Example (.NET and Angular dev container)

```jsonc
{
  "name": "dotnet-angular",
  "build": { "dockerfile": "Dockerfile", "context": ".." },
  "remoteUser": "dev",
  "forwardPorts": [8080, 4200],
  "updateContentCommand": {
    "api": "dotnet restore",
    "ui": "npm ci"
  },
  "postStartCommand": "dotnet --version && node --version"
}
```

The coding assistant and the check tools are installed in the image of `.devcontainer/Dockerfile`; the full template is the folder `templates/containers/devcontainer/`.

The template also installs Poppler (`pdftotext`) and Tesseract (`tesseract`, English and French) for the skill `read-heavy-file`. Set the build argument `EXTRACT_TOOLS` to `"false"` when apt packages cannot be installed; then PDF text and OCR are not available.

## Sources

- [S1] Dev Containers, "Dev Container metadata reference", <https://containers.dev/implementors/json_reference/>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S2] Dev Containers, "Development Container Specification", <https://containers.dev/implementors/spec/>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.

title: Run commands in a dev container
topic: containers
summary: How the coding assistant runs commands in the dev container, the devcontainer CLI, and features without a registry.
tool: devcontainer

# Run commands in a dev container

Contents: 1. The way of this base · 2. Other ways · 3. Features · Sources

## 1. The assistant runs inside the container (way of this base)

The coding assistant is installed in the image of the dev container and runs there. The check commands of `AGENTS.md` are then plain terminal commands: no prefix, no container name.

- Run every check command from the project root, inside the container.
- `remoteUser` and `remoteEnv` of `devcontainer.json` apply, because the assistant is started by the dev container tool.
- If a command is missing, it is missing from the image: say so. Never install it.

## 2. Other ways, from the host (devcontainer CLI and docker exec)

| Way | Command | Limit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Dev Container CLI | `devcontainer up --workspace-folder .` then `devcontainer exec --workspace-folder . <command>` | The CLI must be on the host; `stop` and `down` are not implemented [S2] |
| Container engine | `docker exec <container> <command>` | Does not apply `remoteUser`, `remoteEnv` nor the user environment, which `devcontainer exec` applies [S2] |

- `devcontainer read-configuration --workspace-folder .` prints the configuration of the project: it is the check for `devcontainer.json`. [S2] It first runs `docker ps`: without a container engine that answers, it fails (seen with CLI 0.90.0). Inside the dev container, say that the check needs a rebuild of the container by the user.
- The CLI writes a lock file `.devcontainer-lock.json`; `--frozen-lockfile` enforces it. [S2]
- Tools that read the specification: VS Code, GitHub Codespaces, IntelliJ IDEA (early support), DevPod and the reference CLI. [S3]

## 3. Features, with or without a registry (dev container features)

- A feature is referenced by an OCI registry (`<registry>/<namespace>/<id>[:version]`, for example `ghcr.io/devcontainers/features/go:1`), by an archive address, or by a local folder. [S1]
- A remote feature is downloaded at each build. When the registry cannot be reached, use a `build.dockerfile`, a prebuilt image, or a local feature. [S1]
- A local feature lives in a subfolder of `.devcontainer/` named after its `id`, with `devcontainer-feature.json` and `install.sh`. [S1]

## Sources

- [S1] Dev Containers, "Dev Container Features distribution", <https://containers.dev/implementors/features-distribution/>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S2] devcontainers, "Dev Container CLI", README, <https://github.com/devcontainers/cli>, read 2026-11-12, MIT, summarized.
- [S3] Dev Containers, "Supporting tools and services", <https://containers.dev/supporting.html>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.

tool: hadolint, docker

# Dockerfile checks

Contents: 1. Two tools · 2. Commands · 3. Messages and fixes · 4. Settings · Sources

## 1. Two tools that complete each other (hadolint and docker build --check)

|  | hadolint | `docker build --check` |
| --- | --- | --- |
| What it reads | The Dockerfile and the shell of each `RUN` (ShellCheck rules) | The Dockerfile, with the builder, without building |
| What it needs | Its binary, or its image | Dockerfile syntax 1.8, Buildx 0.15.0, a Docker daemon that answers |
| Sees alone | Missing tag or `latest`, last `USER` root, `ADD`, `pipefail`, package versions | Instruction case, undefined `ARG` and variables, ignored file copied, fixed platform |
| Exit code | Not zero from its failure threshold (default `info`) | Not zero with `--check` |

Both see the exec form, relative `WORKDIR`, secrets in `ARG` or `ENV`, a reserved stage name and a fixed platform. Run hadolint first: it works everywhere. Add the Docker checks when a daemon answers. [S1] [S2] [S3]

Neither tool builds the image. A Dockerfile that passes both can still fail to build. Say so when no build ran. [S1]

## 2. Commands (Dockerfile check commands)

```sh
hadolint Dockerfile
docker info >/dev/null && docker build --check -f Dockerfile .
```

- Without the binary, hadolint runs from its image: `docker run --rm -i hadolint/hadolint < Dockerfile`, or with Podman `podman run --rm -i docker.io/hadolint/hadolint < Dockerfile`. [S3]
- `docker build --check` prints `failed to connect to the docker API` when no daemon answers: skip it and say so. [S1]
- Output formats of hadolint: `-f tty` (default), `json`, `sarif`, `junit` and others. [S3]

## 3. Messages and fixes (hadolint and build check codes)

| Code | Meaning | Fix |
| --- | --- | --- |
| DL3006 | Image without a tag | Add a version tag |
| DL3007 | Image tag `latest` | Write a version tag |
| DL3002 | Last user is root | Add `USER` with a non-root account |
| DL3000, WorkdirRelativePath | Relative `WORKDIR` | Write an absolute path |
| DL3020 | `ADD` for local files | Use `COPY` |
| DL3025, JSONArgsRecommended | `CMD` or `ENTRYPOINT` in shell form | Write the JSON array form |
| DL3008 | System package without a version | Warning only: pin when you know the version |
| DL3009 | Package lists kept | `rm -rf /var/lib/apt/lists/*` in the same `RUN` |
| DL3015 | Recommended packages installed | Add `--no-install-recommends` |
| DL4006 | Pipe without `pipefail` | `SHELL ["/bin/bash", "-o", "pipefail", "-c"]` |
| SecretsUsedInArgOrEnv | A secret name in `ARG` or `ENV` | Remove it from the image |
| CopyIgnoredFile | `COPY` of a file that `.dockerignore` excludes | Fix the copy or the ignore list |
| UndefinedVar, UndefinedArgInFrom | A variable never defined | Declare the `ARG` before it is used |

Sources: [S2] [S3]. Fix only the lines the tool names, then run the same commands again.

## 4. Settings (.hadolint.yaml and check comments)

- `.hadolint.yaml` next to the Dockerfile sets the failure threshold and the level of each rule. Keep DL3008 as a warning. [S3]
- `# hadolint ignore=DL3008` on the line above an instruction skips one rule for that line. Use it only with a reason in the same comment. [S3]
- `# check=skip=<codes>` at the top of the Dockerfile skips build checks; `# check=error=true` makes them fail the build. [S1] [S2]

## Sources

- [S1] Docker, "Checking your build configuration", <https://docs.docker.com/build/checks/>, read 2026-11-12, Apache 2.0, summarized; and a test of the research without a daemon, Docker CLI 29.9.3, Buildx 0.38.1.
- [S2] Docker, "Build checks", <https://docs.docker.com/reference/build-checks/>, read 2026-11-12, Apache 2.0, summarized.
- [S3] hadolint, README, <https://github.com/hadolint/hadolint>, read 2026-11-12, GPL-3.0, rules described in own words.

id: containers-dockerfile-rules
title: Dockerfile rules
topic: containers
summary: Rules with wrong and right examples for a Dockerfile that passes hadolint and the Docker build checks.
tool: docker
version: "1.8"
checked: 2026-11-13
sources: [S1, S2, S3]
license: MIT, written for this base from Apache 2.0 and GPL-3.0 sources, rules described in own words

# Dockerfile rules

Contents: 1. Build and run stages · 2. Base image and user · 3. Instructions · 4. Packages and shell · 5. Context and secrets · Sources

The same rules apply to a `Containerfile` (the name Podman also uses). Check every rule with the tools of `docs/containers/dockerfile-checks.md`.

## 1. Build in one stage, run in another (Dockerfile stages)

Build with the SDK image in a first stage. Copy only the output into a small runtime image. [S1]

Copy the dependency files first and restore them, then copy the rest of the source. The restore layer stays in the cache while the dependency files do not change. [S1]

Wrong:

```dockerfile
FROM mcr.microsoft.com/dotnet/sdk:10.0
COPY . /src
RUN dotnet publish /src/Api/Api.csproj -c Release -o /app
CMD dotnet /app/Api.dll
```

Right:

```dockerfile
# syntax=docker/dockerfile:1
# check=error=true
FROM mcr.microsoft.com/dotnet/sdk:10.0 AS build
WORKDIR /source
COPY Api/Api.csproj Api/
RUN dotnet restore Api/Api.csproj
COPY Api/ Api/
RUN dotnet publish Api/Api.csproj -c Release -o /app --no-restore

FROM mcr.microsoft.com/dotnet/aspnet:10.0 AS final
WORKDIR /app
COPY --from=build /app ./
USER $APP_UID
EXPOSE 8080
ENTRYPOINT ["dotnet", "Api.dll"]
```

`# check=error=true` turns every warning of the Docker build checks into an error. [S2]

## 2. Base image tag and non-root user (Dockerfile FROM and USER)

| Rule | Wrong | Right | Check |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Pin the base image | `FROM ubuntu`, `FROM node:latest` | `FROM node:24` ; a tag and a `@sha256:` digest for a frozen image | hadolint DL3006, DL3007 |
| Run as a non-root user | No `USER`, or `USER root` last | `USER` with a non-root account, by UID when Kubernetes checks `runAsNonRoot` | hadolint DL3002 |
| Write the full image name for Podman | `FROM nginx` | `FROM docker.io/library/nginx:<version>` | Podman refuses a short name without a terminal |

Sources: [S1] [S3]. With Podman, a short name resolves through a list of registries; in `enforcing` mode it fails without a terminal. [S1]

## 3. Instructions (Dockerfile WORKDIR, COPY, CMD and ENTRYPOINT)

| Rule | Wrong | Right | Check |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Exec form for the start command | `CMD dotnet App.dll` | `ENTRYPOINT ["dotnet", "App.dll"]` | JSONArgsRecommended, DL3025 |
| Absolute working folder | `WORKDIR app` | `WORKDIR /app` | WorkdirRelativePath, DL3000 |
| `COPY` rather than `ADD` | `ADD src/ /app` | `COPY src/ /app` | DL3020 |

The exec form passes signals to the process: the container stops cleanly. The shell form starts `/bin/sh` first. [S1] [S2] [S3]

## 4. Packages and shell (Dockerfile RUN)

- Run `apt-get update` and `apt-get install` in the same `RUN`, with `--no-install-recommends`, and remove the package lists in the same `RUN` (DL3015, DL3009). [S1] [S3]
- Put `set -o pipefail &&` before a command with a pipe; otherwise the step hides a failure (DL4006). [S3]
- Version pinning of system packages (DL3008) stays a warning in `.hadolint.yaml`. Never invent a package version number: write the version only when you know it.

Wrong:

```dockerfile
RUN apt-get update
RUN apt-get install -y curl
RUN curl -fsSL https://example.org/tool.tar.gz | tar -xz
```

Right:

```dockerfile
RUN apt-get update \
 && apt-get install -y --no-install-recommends curl \
 && rm -rf /var/lib/apt/lists/*
SHELL ["/bin/bash", "-o", "pipefail", "-c"]
```

## 5. Build context and secrets (Dockerfile .dockerignore, ARG and ENV)

- Keep a `.dockerignore` next to the Dockerfile: `.git`, `**/bin`, `**/obj`, `**/node_modules`, `**/dist`, `.angular`. A file that is ignored but copied triggers the check `CopyIgnoredFile`. [S1] [S2]
- Never put a password, a token or a key in `ARG` or `ENV`: it stays in the image history (SecretsUsedInArgOrEnv). [S2]
- Use `--pull` to refresh the base image and `--no-cache` to rebuild every layer. [S1]

## Sources

- [S1] Docker, "Building best practices", <https://docs.docker.com/build/building/best-practices/>, read 2026-11-12, Apache 2.0, summarized; Red Hat, "Working with container registries", RHEL 10, for short names.
- [S2] Docker, "Build checks" and "Checking your build configuration", <https://docs.docker.com/reference/build-checks/>, read 2026-11-12, Apache 2.0, summarized.
- [S3] hadolint, README, <https://github.com/hadolint/hadolint>, read 2026-11-12, GPL-3.0, rules described in own words.

---
id: containers-dotnet-images
title: .NET container images
topic: containers
summary: Image names, tags, non-root user, port 8080 and image publishing without a Dockerfile for .NET 10.
tool: dotnet
version: "10"
checked: 2026-11-13
sources: [S1, S2, S3, S4, S5]

---

# .NET container images

Contents: 1. Images · 2. Port and user · 3. Dockerfile · 4. Without a Dockerfile · 5. Old patterns · Sources

## 1. Images and base system (.NET images)

- Build with `mcr.microsoft.com/dotnet/sdk:<version>`. Run an ASP.NET Core app with `mcr.microsoft.com/dotnet/aspnet:<version>`. [S1] [S4]
- From .NET 10, the default tags are on Ubuntu 24.04. There are no Debian images for .NET 10. [S5]
- "Chiseled" images are a reduced Ubuntu: no shell, no package manager, a non-root user already active, about 100 MB smaller. Without the `extra` variant they have no culture data. [S1] [S3]
- A chiseled image has no shell: `docker exec <container> sh` fails. Debug with a full image, or read logs from outside.

## 2. Port and user (.NET images since .NET 8)

| Point | Value | Source |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Port | 8080 by default since .NET 8 (80 before) | [S1] [S3] |
| Variable for the port | `ASPNETCORE_HTTP_PORTS` | [S3] |
| User | Non-root account `app`, UID 1654, in every image since .NET 8 | [S1] [S3] |
| Variable for the user | `APP_UID` | [S2] [S3] |

- Going back to port 80 forbids the non-root user: ports under 1024 need root. Keep 8080. [S3]
- Kubernetes checks `runAsNonRoot` with a UID, not a name: write `USER $APP_UID`, not `USER app`. [S3]

## 3. A .NET Dockerfile (.NET image build)

```dockerfile
# syntax=docker/dockerfile:1
# check=error=true
ARG DOTNET_VERSION=10.0
FROM mcr.microsoft.com/dotnet/sdk:${DOTNET_VERSION} AS build
WORKDIR /source
COPY Api/Api.csproj Api/
RUN dotnet restore Api/Api.csproj
COPY Api/ Api/
RUN dotnet publish Api/Api.csproj -c Release -o /app --no-restore

FROM mcr.microsoft.com/dotnet/aspnet:${DOTNET_VERSION} AS final
WORKDIR /app
COPY --from=build /app ./
USER $APP_UID
EXPOSE 8080
ENTRYPOINT ["dotnet", "Api.dll"]
```

Read the .NET version of the project in `TargetFramework` (`net10.0` gives `10.0`) and set `DOTNET_VERSION`. The full template is `templates/containers/dotnet.Dockerfile`. [S4]

## 4. Publish an image without a Dockerfile (dotnet publish PublishContainer)

```sh
dotnet publish --os linux --arch x64 /t:PublishContainer -p ContainerImageTag=1.4.0
dotnet publish --os linux --arch x64 /t:PublishContainer -p ContainerArchiveOutputPath=./images/api.tar.gz
```

- The first form needs a container daemon that answers; without one it fails with MSB4018 (task `CreateNewImage`). The second writes an archive and needs no daemon. Load it with `docker load -i` or `podman load -i`. [S2]
- Push to a registry with `-p ContainerRegistry=<registry>`. [S2]
- The default tag is `latest`. Always set `ContainerImageTag` to a version. [S2]
- Other properties: `ContainerRepository` (default: assembly name), `ContainerFamily`, `ContainerBaseImage`, `ContainerUser`, `ContainerPort`, `ContainerEnvironmentVariable`. `ContainerEntrypoint` is deprecated since .NET 8: use `ContainerAppCommand`. [S2]

## 5. Old patterns (.NET images before .NET 8 and 10)

| Old | Now | Since |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `EXPOSE 80`, `ASPNETCORE_URLS` set to port 80 | `EXPOSE 8080`, `ASPNETCORE_HTTP_PORTS` | .NET 8 |
| Image runs as root | `USER $APP_UID` | .NET 8 |
| Tags `-bookworm-slim` (Debian) | Default tags (Ubuntu 24.04) | .NET 10 |
| `ContainerEntrypoint` | `ContainerAppCommand` | .NET 8 |

## Sources

- [S1] Microsoft, ".NET containers overview", <https://learn.microsoft.com/dotnet/core/docker/introduction>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S2] Microsoft, "Containerize a .NET app with dotnet publish" and "Containerize a .NET app reference", <https://learn.microsoft.com/dotnet/core/containers/sdk-publish>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S3] Microsoft, "What's new in containers for .NET 8", <https://learn.microsoft.com/dotnet/core/whats-new/dotnet-8/containers>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S4] Microsoft, "Building .NET Docker images", <https://learn.microsoft.com/dotnet/core/docker/building-net-docker-images>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, adapted.
- [S5] Microsoft, "Default .NET images use Ubuntu", <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/core/compatibility/containers/10.0/default-images-use-ubuntu>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.

id: containers-podman-quadlet-kube

topic: containers
summary: Run a container as a systemd service with Quadlet, and run a Kubernetes YAML locally with podman kube play.

# Podman Quadlet and kube play

Contents: 1. Quadlet · 2. kube play · 3. Pitfalls · Sources

## 1. A container as a service (Podman Quadlet)

- A file `.container` (or `.volume`, `.network`, `.kube`, `.image`, `.build`, `.pod`) becomes a systemd service after `systemctl daemon-reload`. [S1]
- Folders: `/etc/containers/systemd/` for root, `~/.config/containers/systemd/` for a user. [S1]
- `api.container` gives the service `api.service`; the container is named `systemd-api`. [S1]
- The generated services are transient: `systemctl enable` does not apply. Start at boot with an `[Install]` section and `WantedBy=default.target`. [S1]
- Dry run of the generator: `/usr/lib/systemd/system-generators/podman-system-generator --user --dryrun`. [S1]
- Pulling an image can take longer than the 90 seconds systemd allows: set `TimeoutStartSec` or pull the image first. [S1]

```ini
# ~/.config/containers/systemd/api.container
[Container]
Image=registry.example.internal/api:1.4.0
PublishPort=8080:8080

[Service]
TimeoutStartSec=300

[Install]
WantedBy=default.target
```

## 2. A Kubernetes YAML on one machine (podman kube play)

- `podman kube play app.yaml` creates the containers, pods and volumes of the file; `--down` removes them; `--replace` creates them again. [S2]
- Kinds it handles: Pod, Deployment, PersistentVolumeClaim, ConfigMap, Secret, DaemonSet, Job. Service and Ingress are not handled. [S2]
- Fields it does not handle, among others: `imagePullSecrets`, `readinessProbe`, `startupProbe`. [S2]
- With the tag `:latest`, Podman tries to pull the image. [S2]
- It expects best a file made by `podman kube generate`. [S2]

`podman kube play` runs a Pod locally. It does not validate a manifest for a cluster: use `docs/kubernetes/validate-offline.md` for that.

## 3. Pitfalls (Quadlet and kube play)

- `systemctl enable api.service` for a Quadlet unit: it fails. Use the `[Install]` section.
- A short image name in `Image=`: write the full name with its registry.
- A Service or an Ingress in the file for `kube play`: Podman ignores them; publish ports in the Pod instead.

## Sources

- [S1] Podman, "podman-systemd.unit(5)", v6.1.0, <https://docs.podman.io/en/v6.1.0/markdown/podman-systemd.unit.5.html>, read 2026-11-12, Apache 2.0, summarized.
- [S2] Podman, "podman-kube-play(1)", <https://docs.podman.io/en/stable/markdown/podman-kube-play.1.html>, read 2026-11-12, Apache 2.0, summarized.

summary: What changes with Podman, with full image names, rootless ports, pasta network, compose provider and registry mirrors.

# Podman compared with Docker

Contents: 1. What differs · 2. Same commands · 3. Registries and mirrors · 4. Pitfalls · Sources

## 1. What differs with Podman (defaults that make a command fail)

| Point | Podman behavior | What to write |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Image names | A short name resolves through a list of registries. In `enforcing` mode, without a terminal, the command fails. | Always the full name: `docker.io/library/nginx:<version>`, never `nginx`. [S3] |
| Rootless ports | Ports under 1024 are refused (kernel setting `net.ipv4.ip_unprivileged_port_start`). | Publish a high port: `-p 8080:8080`. [S1] |
| Rootless limits | 65,536 user ids only; no device creation; home folder on NFS not handled. | Do not assume root in the container. [S1] |
| Rootless network | "pasta" by default since Podman 5.0: the container does not reach the main address of the host; the default port forwarder hides the client address. | Never write the host address in a configuration. [S1] |
| Compose | `podman compose` passes its arguments to an external provider: `docker-compose` if installed, else `podman-compose`. Set it with `compose_providers` or `PODMAN_COMPOSE_PROVIDER`. | Install a provider next to Podman. [S2] |
| Build file | Podman also reads a file named `Containerfile`. | Treat both names the same. |

## 2. Same commands (Docker and Podman equivalents)

| Need | Docker | Podman |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Run a tool in a container | `docker run --rm -i hadolint/hadolint < Dockerfile` | `podman run --rm -i docker.io/hadolint/hadolint < Dockerfile` |
| Load an image archive | `docker load -i image.tar.gz` | `podman load -i image.tar.gz` |
| Publish a .NET image with the SDK | The daemon is found | The same, no extra setting |

Only these three lines were checked in primary sources. For other commands, run `podman <command> --help` before you write an option. [S1] [S2]

## 3. Registries and mirrors (Podman registries.conf)

- Registries and mirrors are set in `registries.conf`: one `[[registry]]` entry, then its `[[registry.mirror]]` entries, tried in order. `insecure=true` allows a local registry without TLS. [S3]
- Example:

```toml
[[registry]]
prefix = "docker.io"
location = "docker.io"

[[registry.mirror]]
location = "registry.example.internal:5000"
insecure = true
```

Write the real mirror of the team in the `AGENTS.md` of the project, not in this base.

## 4. Pitfalls of a model with Podman

- A short image name in a `podman` command, a Quadlet file or a `FROM`.
- Port 80 or 443 published from a rootless container.
- `systemctl enable` for a Quadlet service (see `docs/containers/podman-quadlet-kube.md`).
- `podman compose` or `docker build --check` used as if nothing else were needed.
- A success of `podman kube play` taken as proof that a manifest is right for a cluster.

## Sources

- [S1] Podman, "podman-rootless(7)", package 6.2.0, <https://man.archlinux.org/man/podman-rootless.7.en>, read 2026-11-12, Apache 2.0, summarized.
- [S2] Podman, "podman-compose(1)", <https://docs.podman.io/en/stable/markdown/podman-compose.1.html>, read 2026-11-12, Apache 2.0, summarized.
- [S3] Red Hat, "Working with container registries", RHEL 10, <https://docs.redhat.com/en/documentation/red_hat_enterprise_linux/10/html/building_running_and_managing_containers/working-with-container-registries>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.

summary: Which apiVersion to write for each kind, and the versions removed from 1.16 to 1.32. Kubernetes 1.37.
