=====BASE-EXPORT-1-571e463b6744===== FILE 1/1 9102a3f1597041e887ee9adcb6c640a8991a25820e64848d4841de2c02379259 RAW chat/knowledge/synoptics/synoptic-rules.md
---
id: synoptics-synoptic-rules
title: Synoptic display rules
topic: synoptics
summary: Rules to lay out a synoptic view and to show equipment states, alerts and flashing.
tool: none
version: "none"
checked: 2026-11-12
sources: [S1, S2, S4, S5, S6, S11, S12, S14, S16, S32]
license: MIT (own words; standards and guides are cited by reference, never copied)
variant: chat
---

# Synoptic display rules

Contents: 1. Layout · 2. Colour · 3. Equipment states · 4. Alerts · 5. Flashing and motion · 6. Text and numbers · 7. Final check · Sources

## 1. Layout (synoptic view)

- Show only what the operator's task needs. Draw no decoration, no 3D effect, no logo. [S2]
- Label every component. Start and end every flow line at a label or at a labelled component. [S2]
- Mark the flow direction with arrowheads. [S2]
- Do not overlap lines. Draw each crossing so that it cannot be read as a junction. [S2]
- Make what changes (a valve position, a value) stand out more than what never changes (a pipe, a tank outline). [S2]
- Put each value next to the equipment it belongs to. [S2]
- Give every view a short title at the top. [S2]
- Keep the view sparse. Use at most half of the area. Use at most a quarter on a view that is mostly text. [S2]
- Keep the same place for the same thing on every view. [S6]
- Build four levels of views and link them: overview, system, equipment detail, diagnostic. Put no command on the overview. [S1]

## 2. Colour (synoptic view)

- Use a plain background with no pattern. [S2]
- Provide a day palette and a night palette. At night, draw light items on a dark background. [S6]
- Take every colour from a CSS variable. Never write a colour value inside a symbol.
- Use few colours. Give one meaning to one colour. [S2]
- Draw equipment, pipes and normal values in neutral greys. Use bright colour only for an abnormal situation. [S1]
- Reserve red, orange and yellow for alerts and for invalid data. [S5] [S6]
- Never use red or green to mean open or closed, running or stopped. People from different trades read them in opposite ways. [S2]
- Never use colour alone. Add a shape, a pattern or a word that gives the same information. [S2] [S6] [S14]
- Keep a contrast of at least 3:1 between a graphic part and what touches it. Keep at least 4.5:1 for text. [S14]
- In a normal situation, highlight at most about one tenth of the view. [S2]

Wrong:

```css
.syn-valve[data-position="open"] .syn-shape { fill: green; }
.syn-valve[data-position="closed"] .syn-shape { fill: red; }
```

Right:

```css
.syn-valve[data-position="open"] .syn-shape { fill: var(--syn-fill-active); }
.syn-valve[data-position="closed"] .syn-shape { fill: var(--syn-fill-inactive); }
/* The symbol also shows the word OPEN or CLOSED. */
```

## 3. Equipment states (synoptic symbol)

Show each state with a fill or a shape, and with a word. Use this table.

| State | Fill or shape | Word or badge |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Open, running, on | Bright fill | `OPEN`, `RUN`, `ON` |
| Closed, stopped, off | Dark fill | `CLOSED`, `STOP`, `OFF` |
| Opening, closing, starting, stopping | Hatched fill | `OPENING`, `CLOSING`, `STARTING`, `STOPPING` |
| Partly open | Bright fill | The position, for example `45 %` |
| Unknown position, invalid value | No fill, dashed outline | `?` |
| Fault, or position different from the command | Alert badge (section 4) | The alert text |
| Local control | Normal fill | Badge `LOC` |
| Manual mode | Normal fill | Badge `MAN` |
| Out of service, locked out | Cross-hatch over the symbol | Badge `OOS` |
| Inhibited, interlocked | Normal fill | Badge `INH` |
| Simulated or forced value | Normal fill | Badge `SIM` |
| Device diagnostic | Normal fill | Badge `FAIL`, `CHECK`, `OFFSPEC` or `MAINT` |

- Use only two brightness levels: bright and dark. [S2]
- Show a mode badge only when the mode is not the normal mode of the equipment.
- Use the four standard diagnostic cases: failure, function check, out of specification, maintenance required. A forced or substitute value is a function check. Local operation is a function check. [S16]
- Never show an invalid or doubtful value as a good value. Mark it. [S6]
- Write the words in the language of the project screens. The words above are the English defaults.

## 4. Alerts (synoptic symbol and alert list)

Use four priorities. [S4] [S5]

| Priority | Meaning | Not acknowledged | Acknowledged | Sound |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `emergency` | Immediate danger to life or to the ship | Flashing | Steady | Follow the emergency signal rules of the project |
| `alarm` | Needs attention and action now | Flashing | Steady until the cause is gone | Until acknowledged |
| `warning` | Needs attention now, no action yet | Flashing | Steady until the cause is gone | One short signal |
| `caution` | Needs more attention than usual | Steady | Steady | None |

- Show an alert with a badge next to the equipment that raised it. [S1]
- Give each priority its own outer shape, its own letter and its own colour. Defaults: `E` in an octagon, red; `A` in a square, red; `W` in a triangle, orange; `C` in a circle, yellow. [S1] [S2]
- Draw a dark border around every badge. Orange and yellow do not contrast enough with a light background.
- Use the alert colours and the badge shapes for alerts only. [S1] [S2]
- Acknowledge alerts one by one. Never acknowledge a group in one action. [S4]
- For a shelved, suppressed or out-of-service alarm: show no alert colour and no flashing. A small neutral marker is allowed. Provide a list of these alarms. [S11] [S12]
- In the alert list: show at least 20 alerts at once. Sort by priority, then by time. Keep a history with the times raised, acknowledged and cleared. [S4]
- From an alert, open the view of its source in one action. [S11]

## 5. Flashing and motion (synoptic symbol)

- Flash only an alarm or a warning that is not acknowledged. Flash nothing else. [S4] [S6]
- Use one rate: 1 Hz, bright for half of the period. Stay between 0.5 Hz and 1.5 Hz. [S5]
- Never flash more than three times per second. [S14]
- Flash the badge only. Never flash a text that must be read. Never flash the whole symbol. [S2]
- Flash between two visible levels. Never hide the item. [S2]
- Flash all the badges of a view in phase, from one clock.
- When the user asks for reduced motion, stop the flashing and draw a thick border instead. [S14] [S32]
- Add no other animation: no rotating pump, no moving fluid, no growing bar. [S2]

## 6. Text and numbers (synoptic symbol)

- Write every label and every number horizontally, even when the symbol is rotated. [S2]
- Put the label close to its symbol. [S2]
- Use the same label for the same item on every view. [S2]
- Spell words out. Abbreviate only when space forces it. Never use one abbreviation for two words. [S2]
- Show each value with its unit. Use the same unit for the same quantity on every view. [S6]
- Show only the digits the task needs. [S2]
- Refresh a value that must be read at most once per second. [S2]
- Add an arrow when the operator must see the direction of change. [S2]

## 7. Final check (synoptic view)

- Is every colour information doubled by a shape, a pattern or a word?
- Is red or green used for open, closed, running or stopped? It must not be.
- Does anything flash other than an unacknowledged alarm or warning? It must not.
- Does every colour come from a CSS variable?
- Does every flow line start and end at a label or at a labelled component?

## Sources

- [S1] B. R. Hollifield, "The High Performance HMI", ISA Interchange, undated, <https://blog.isa.org/the-high-performance-hmi>, read 2026-11-12, license not stated, summarized.
- [S2] U.S. NRC, "Human-System Interface Design Review Guidelines", NUREG-0700 Rev. 3, 2020, sections 1.2.8, 1.3.2 to 1.3.10, 1.4, 1.5, <https://www.nrc.gov/docs/ML2016/ML20162A214.pdf>, read 2026-11-12, U.S. federal publication, summarized.
- [S4] IMO, Resolution MSC.302(87), "Performance standards for bridge alert management", 2010, <https://wwwcdn.imo.org/localresources/en/KnowledgeCentre/IndexofIMOResolutions/MSCResolutions/MSC.302(87).pdf>, read 2026-11-12, license not stated, summarized.
- [S5] IMO, Resolution A.1021(26), "Code on Alerts and Indicators, 2009", <https://wwwcdn.imo.org/localresources/en/KnowledgeCentre/IndexofIMOResolutions/AssemblyDocuments/A.1021(26).pdf>, read 2026-11-12, license not stated, summarized.
- [S6] IMO, Resolution MSC.191(79), "Performance standards for the presentation of navigation-related information on shipborne navigational displays", 2004, <https://wwwcdn.imo.org/localresources/en/KnowledgeCentre/IndexofIMOResolutions/MSCResolutions/MSC.191(79).pdf>, read 2026-11-12, license not stated, summarized.
- [S11] Siemens Industry, "Setting a new standard in alarm management", 2021, <https://assets.new.siemens.com/siemens/assets/api/uuid:234f1026-298f-49dc-8961-0c5223c38588/white-paper-alarm-management-2021-final.pdf>, read 2026-11-12, all rights reserved, summarized.
- [S12] Siemens Ltd, "Improving alarm management with ISA-18.2: Part 2", Process Online, 2014, <https://www.processonline.com.au/content/software-it/article/improving-alarm-management-with-isa-18-2-part-2-1141142642>, read 2026-11-12, license not stated, summarized.
- [S14] W3C, "Web Content Accessibility Guidelines (WCAG) 2.2", Recommendation of 2024-12-12, <https://www.w3.org/TR/WCAG22/>, read 2026-11-12, W3C Document License, summarized.
- [S16] OPC Foundation, "OPC 10000-100: Devices", v1.05, section 4.5.4, <https://reference.opcfoundation.org/specs/OPC-10000-100/4.5.4>, read 2026-11-12, OPC Foundation license, summarized.
- [S32] MDN, "prefers-reduced-motion", <https://developer.mozilla.org/docs/Web/CSS/Reference/At-rules/@media/prefers-reduced-motion>, read 2026-11-12, license not read, summarized.

title: FMI, SSP and co-simulation

summary: FMI 3.0 and SSP 2.0.1, with FMU content, interface types, variables, co-simulation step and pitfalls.

# FMI, SSP and co-simulation

Contents: 1. FMI · 2. SSP · 3. Co-simulation · 4. When to use · Sources

## 1. FMI 3.0 (Functional Mock-up Interface)

- An FMU is a ZIP archive: the XML description (`modelDescription.xml`), compiled libraries or C sources, and data. [S1]
- Three interface types: Model Exchange (the solver is in the importer), Co-Simulation (the model usually brings its solver), Scheduled Execution (an outer scheduler activates partitions of the model, for example on a real-time platform). [S1]
- Variables have a causality (parameter, input, output, local, independent) and a variability: see `docs/simulation/component-models.md`. [S1]
- It exchanges a "black box" model between tools and protects intellectual property; it was designed for continuous systems. [S1] [S3]

## 2. SSP 2.0.1 (System Structure and Parameterization)

A tool-neutral format to describe, package and exchange the structure of a system of components, FMUs among them, and its parameter sets. [S2] [S4]

## 3. Co-simulation (orchestration and pitfalls)

- An orchestrator advances the time of each unit and carries outputs to inputs. [S3]
- Jacobi: all units exchange at the same instant and advance in parallel. Gauss-Seidel: they advance in a set order, more accurate, less parallel. [S3]
- Pitfalls: algebraic loops, extrapolation of inputs between two exchanges, no rollback for a black box. [S3]
- Maritime: the Open Simulation Platform (DNV, Kongsberg Maritime, SINTEF Ocean, NTNU) co-simulates independent models with FMI, can describe the structure with SSP, and adds its own interface specification (variable groups, unit conversion, checks before the run). [S5]

## 4. When to use FMI and SSP

| Need | Choice |
| --- | --- |
| Receive or deliver the model of a piece of equipment without its code | FMI |
| Describe the assembly of several FMUs and their parameters | SSP |
| Models written in the same simulator code | Neither: the model interface of the simulator |

## Sources

- [S1] Modelica Association, "Functional Mock-up Interface Specification", version 3.0, 2022-05-10, <https://fmi-standard.org/docs/3.0>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA 4.0, summarized.
- [S2] Modelica Association Project SSP, "System Structure and Parameterization", version 2.0.1, <https://ssp-standard.org/>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S3] Gomes, Thule, Broman, Larsen, Vangheluwe, "Co-simulation: State of the art", arXiv:1702.00686, 2017, <https://ar5iv.labs.arxiv.org/html/1702.00686>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S4] Modelica Association Project SSP, "ssp-standard", repository, <https://github.com/modelica/ssp-standard>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA 4.0, summarized.
- [S5] Open Simulation Platform, "OSP on GitHub", <https://open-simulation-platform.github.io/>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.

# Component model interface

Contents: 1. Variable roles · 2. Life cycle · 3. C# template · 4. Link with symbol properties · Sources

## 1. Variable roles of a model (FMI 3.0 causality)

| Role | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `parameter` | Given before the run; constant, unless tunable |
| `calculatedParameter` | Computed at initialisation or when a tunable parameter changes |
| `input` | Given by the importer, before the step |
| `output` | Used by the importer, after the step |
| `local` | Internal (continuous state, debug value); never linked to another model |
| `independent` | The independent variable, usually time; one per model |

Variability says when a value can change: `constant`, `fixed`, `tunable`, `discrete`, `continuous`. [S1] ECSS SMP says the same with state, input and output flags on each published field. [S2]

## 2. Life cycle, seen by three references

| Step | FMI 3.0 [S1] | ECSS SMP [S2] | Trick [S3] [S4] |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Create and describe | Instantiation; `modelDescription.xml` | `Publish`, `Configure` | Default data, input file |
| Connect | The importer links outputs to inputs | `Connect`, link registry | Simulation definition file |
| Initialise | Initialisation mode | Initialising state | Initialisation jobs |
| Step | `fmi3DoStep`, or derivatives to an outer solver | Entry points called by the scheduler | Derivative, integration, scheduled jobs |
| Save, restore | Get, set, serialize the FMU state | Storing and restoring states | Jobs around the checkpoint |
| Reset, end | `fmi3Reset`, free the instance | Exiting, aborting | Shutdown jobs |

## 3. C# template of a component model

The smallest interface that covers the three references: parameters set before the run, inputs written before the step, a step that reads neither the clock nor the network, outputs read after the step, a full state that can be read and written, faults and forcings inside the state.

```csharp
public interface IModel
{
    void Initialize();
    void Step(long stepTicks);
    ModelState Save();
    void Restore(ModelState state);
}

public sealed record PumpParameters(double RatedFlowM3PerH, double StartTimeS);

public sealed record PumpState(double FlowRateM3PerH, double StartElapsedS, bool HasTripFault);

public sealed class PumpModel(PumpParameters parameters) : IModel
{
    private PumpState state = new(0, 0, false);

    public bool IsRunCommanded { get; set; }
    public double FlowRateM3PerH => state.FlowRateM3PerH;

    public void Initialize() => state = new PumpState(0, 0, false);

    public void Step(long stepTicks)
    {
        double dt = stepTicks / (double)TimeSpan.TicksPerSecond;
        bool running = IsRunCommanded && !state.HasTripFault;
        double elapsed = running ? state.StartElapsedS + dt : 0;
        double ratio = Math.Min(1.0, elapsed / parameters.StartTimeS);
        state = state with { StartElapsedS = elapsed, FlowRateM3PerH = parameters.RatedFlowM3PerH * ratio };
    }

    public ModelState Save() => ModelState.From(state);

    public void Restore(ModelState saved) => state = saved.To<PumpState>();
}
```

`ModelState` stands for the serialization of the project; the instructor sets `HasTripFault` through a restore or a fault command.

## 4. Link with the symbol properties (synoptics)

What the operator sets is an input; what he reads is an output; what describes the equipment is a parameter; what the instructor breaks is a fault. Name them with `docs/synoptics/symbol-property-naming.md`; the catalogs give the properties of each equipment.

## Sources

- [S1] Modelica Association, "Functional Mock-up Interface Specification", version 3.0, 2022-05-10, <https://fmi-standard.org/docs/3.0>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA 4.0, summarized.
- [S2] ECSS, "Simulation modelling platform", ECSS-E-ST-40-07C Rev.1, 2025-08-05, <https://ecss.nl/wp-content/uploads/2025/08/ECSS-E-ST-40-07C-Rev.1(5August2025).pdf>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S3] NASA, Trick documentation, "Executive Scheduler", <https://nasa.github.io/trick/documentation/simulation_capabilities/Executive-Scheduler>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S4] Penn, Lin, "The Trick Simulation Toolkit", NASA NTRS 20150021786, <https://ntrs.nasa.gov/api/citations/20150021786/downloads/20150021786.pdf>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.

# HLA, DIS and DDS

Contents: 1. Compared · 2. What each brings · 3. When to use · Sources

## 1. HLA, DIS and DDS compared

| Criterion | HLA (IEEE 1516-2025) | DIS (IEEE 1278.1-2012) | DDS 1.4 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| What it links | Simulators and tools (federates) in a federation, through a RTI | Platform simulators, in real time | The applications of one system |
| Data model | Set by the federation (FOM, XML), modular | Fixed: 72 message types (PDUs) in 13 families | Typed topics |
| Time | Time management services: regulating or constrained federate, lookahead, time advance request and grant | Real time; dead reckoning between two messages | Not in the pages read |
| Freeze, save | Synchronization points, federation save and restore | Start, resume, stop, freeze messages | Not in the pages read |
| Transport | Depends on the RTI (TCP or multicast) | UDP broadcast, multicast | Peer-to-peer publish-subscribe, discovery, quality of service |
| Bodies | IEEE, SISO | IEEE, SISO | OMG |

Sources: [S1] [S2] [S3] [S4] [S5] [S6]. HLA and DIS texts are paid and were not read.

## 2. What each one brings (distributed simulation)

- HLA: seven service groups (federation, declaration, object, ownership, time, data distribution, support) and ten rules; recommended by NATO (STANAG 4603); HLA 4 approved by IEEE in early 2025, with security, scaling and cloud readiness announced. [S1] [S2]
- DIS: from SIMNET; a fixed object model where each simulator broadcasts the state of its entities (Entity State, Fire, Detonation); the RPR FOM maps DIS messages to HLA classes; dead reckoning predicts a position from the last known state and cuts traffic. [S2] [S3] [S4]
- DDS: data-centric middleware; each application reads and writes a local data space, synchronized peer to peer; quality of service sets reliability, liveliness, filtering and deadlines. [S5] [S6]
- Known limit of HLA: no interface for end-to-end deadlines nor system priorities; its callback wait is a source of non-determinism; research adds fixed-priority scheduling, memory locking and CPU affinity for hard real time. [S7]

## 3. When to use each (distributed simulation)

| Need | Choice |
| --- | --- |
| Link the simulator to those of other teams or nations, with a common logical time | HLA |
| Exchange positions and events with existing simulators in real time, without logical time | DIS |
| Distribute data between applications of one system, with delivery guarantees | DDS |
| Let the models of one simulator talk, in one process | None: a function call and a table of variables |

## Sources

- [S1] SISO, "HLA 4 Approved by IEEE", 2025-03-11, <https://www.sisostandards.org/news/695782/HLA-4-Approved-by-IEEE.htm>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S2] Wikipedia, "High Level Architecture", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High_Level_Architecture>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA 4.0, summarized.
- [S3] Wikipedia, "Distributed Interactive Simulation", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Distributed_Interactive_Simulation>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA 4.0, summarized.
- [S4] Wikipedia, "Dead reckoning", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dead_reckoning>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA 4.0, summarized.
- [S5] DDS Foundation (OMG), "What is DDS?", <https://www.dds-foundation.org/what-is-dds-3/>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S6] OpenDDS Foundation, "Introduction to DDS", <https://opendds.readthedocs.io/en/master/devguide/introduction_to_dds.html>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S7] Adelantado, Siron, Chaudron, "Towards an HLA Run-time Infrastructure with Hard Real-time Capabilities", paper 10E-SIW-011, <https://cdimage.debian.org/mirror/gnu.org/savannah/certi/papers/10E-SIW-011.pdf>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.

# Testing a simulation model

Contents: 1. Tests from the references · 2. Tests of a training simulator · 3. Verification and validation · Sources

This document says what to test in a model. To write and run the tests in C#, read `docs/dotnet-testing/unit-test-rules.md` and `docs/dotnet-testing/run-tests.md`; for the clock, `FakeTimeProvider` in `docs/dotnet-testing/integration-tests.md`.

## 1. Tests from the references (model testing)

| Test | What it checks |
| --- | --- |
| Unit test | One component alone, for example a consistent initialisation [S4] |
| Numerical reference | The result is compared to a kept reference, with absolute and relative tolerances [S4] |
| Order of convergence | The error falls as expected when the step gets finer [S4] |
| Step convergence | Two runs, the second finer: no significant difference [S1] |
| Comparison with another model | The results follow an analytic model or a valid simulation model [S3] |
| Degenerate and extreme conditions | The model stays logical with an extreme value or an unlikely combination [S3] |
| Sensitivity | The outputs answer a change of input as the real system would [S3] |
| Non-regression | Representative runs are played again after each change [S5] |

## 2. Tests of a training simulator (model testing)

Three tests follow from the time rules and the training functions:

- Determinism: the same inputs twice give the same outputs, step by step.
- Save and restore: save, restore, go on; the outputs equal those of a run without a cut.
- Freeze: freeze, wait, run again; the outputs do not change.

```csharp
[Fact]
public void Restore_then_step_gives_the_same_outputs_as_an_unbroken_run()
{
    PumpModel unbroken = NewRunningPump();
    PumpModel restored = NewRunningPump();
    Step(unbroken, 10);
    Step(restored, 5);
    ModelState saved = restored.Save();
    restored.Restore(saved);
    Step(restored, 5);
    Assert.Equal(unbroken.FlowRateM3PerH, restored.FlowRateM3PerH);
}
```

## 3. Verification and validation (V&V of a model)

- Verification: the program implements the model correctly; validation: the model represents the real system accurately enough for its use. [S2]
- Set the required accuracy of the outputs before development; compare with the real system on several sets of conditions; write it down; review it periodically. [S2]
- Acceptance of a training simulator: tests against plant reference data, with acceptance criteria, initial conditions and expected operator actions; integrated, malfunction and beyond-design groups; pre-factory, factory and site steps. [S5]

## Sources

- [S1] MathWorks, "Check and Improve Simulation Accuracy", Simulink documentation, <https://au.mathworks.com/help/simulink/ug/improving-simulation-accuracy.html>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S2] Sargent, "An Introductory Tutorial on Verification and Validation of Simulation Models", Winter Simulation Conference 2015, <https://www.informs-sim.org/wsc15papers/182.pdf>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S3] Sargent, "Verification and Validation of Simulation Models", Winter Simulation Conference 2005, <https://www.informs-sim.org/wsc05papers/014.pdf>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.

- [S5] IAEA, "Guidelines for upgrade and modernization of nuclear power plant training simulators", IAEA-TECDOC-1500, 2006, <https://www-pub.iaea.org/MTCD/Publications/PDF/te_1500_web.pdf>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
